Trade Tags 
For a game master to be able to adjudicate trade they must be 
familiar with trade tags. Trade tags represent the different qualities 
of settlements within the game world and help determine what 
goods are available and at what price. When the players first 
encounter a settlement within the campaign world with the 
intention of engaging in trade there then the game master should 
choose between 2-4 trade tags from below, which best describe the 
settlement. 
 
Trade Tag Descriptions 
▶ Agricultural (Ag): The settlement and nearby area is dotted with 
many farms. 
▶ City (Ci): The settlement is a city or other large metropolitan 
area. Mutually exclusive with the town and village tags. 
▶ Dwarf (Dw): The settlement is a dwarf settlement. 
▶ Educated (Ed): The settlement has a strong need for intellectual 
material like books and writing material. 
▶ Frontier (Fr): The settlement is very far removed from other 
settlements. 
▶ Refined (Re): The residents of this settlement have a taste for 
the finer things in life.  
▶ Rivers/Canals (Rc): The settlement well-connected by rivers and 
canals. 
▶ Town (To): The settlement is a medium-sized town. Mutually 
exclusive with the village and city tags. 
▶ Village (Vi): The settlement is a small village. Mutually exclusive 
with the town and city tags. 
▶ Wilderness (Wi): The settlement is surrounded by wilderness 
such as woods, swamps or hills.  
 
 
 


 
64 
 
Invocations and Blessings
Anyone can appeal to a deity for a blessing but some religious 
characters in the world may be imbued with holy power, granting 
them the ability to invoke their god’s powers directly. The most 
powerful of such characters may one day hope to perform miracles: 
explosive and inexplicable displays of deific power. Both prayers 
and miracles are collectively called ‘invocations’. 
 
Invocations and Blessings 
Invocations are direct expressions of a god’s power and may only 
be expressed by those who have learned the specific words and 
rituals associated with prayers and miracles. 
 
Blessings are the expression of a god’s power within the world 
indirectly. It is unclear to a layperson whether a blessing has really 
been performed or not. When a blessing does happen, it is often a 
minor, deniable expression of a god’s power. 
 
The Difference Between Prayers and Miracles 
Prayers and miracles are both types of ‘invocations’ expressed by 
calling on a specific deity. However, their expressions within the 
world vary greatly. Many layfolk may be able to utter a prayer and 
invoke a minor effect, which is a lesser manifestation of a god’s 
power. The effects of prayers are often subtle, and it is often 
impossible for those without mage sight to see that anything 
supernatural has taken place at all. Miracles on the other hand are 
explicit expressions of a god’s will within the world. Miracles may 
take the form of smiting bolts of power or the healing of 
supplicants with the most extreme wounds. Miracles are also given 
by particular gods and usually a character may only ever learn to 
invoke the miracles of a single god – trying to prove their loyalty to 
multiple deities is challenging for a single mortal! 
 
Invoking Prayers and Miracles 
To invoke a prayer or a miracle a character must have the 
appropriate talent, have learned the invocation and then must 
successfully make a Pray test, which requires an action. In combat, 
all invocations are activated during the Supernatural Abilities step. 
Miracles cost an amount of FP to invoke, whereas invocations do 
not cost FP. If a character attempts to invoke a miracle, the listed FP 
is only spent if the Pray test is successful. 
 
Reading Invocation Entries 
When an invocation is invoked, whether successfully or 
unsuccessfully, consult the entry for the invocations. There are four 
entries for each invocations: critical failure (if a natural 1 was rolled 
on the Pray test); a failure; a success and a critical success (if a 
natural 20 was rolled on the Pray test). 
 
Each invocation will also have a number of entries explained below: 
▶ Fails: The invocation fails and does not produce any desired 
effect. If the entry includes this, then the FP cost of the invocation is 
not used.  
▶ Minor Disfavour: The character, perhaps for some unforeseeable 
reason, irritated their god. They must immediately roll on the Minor 
Divine Disfavour table, suffering the effect. 
▶ Major Disfavour: The character has offended their patron deity 
in some deeply taboo way. They must immediately roll on the Major 
Divine Disfavour table, suffering the effect. 
 
Miracle FP Cost 
In the title of a miracle is a listed FP cost. If the character invokes 
a miracle with a successful pray test, then the character must 
immediately spend this listed FP or the miracle automatically fails. 
 
Durations, Targets and Spheres 
▶ All invocations will list their legitimate targets. Such ranges are 
given in feet or apply only to the priest themselves. 
▶ All invocations will list durations; this will be given explicitly but 
may vary depending on the level of the caster. 
 
 
 
Divine Disfavour 
When a character rolls a minor or major divine disfavour entry 
on an invocation table, they must immediately roll on the 
appropriate table below, suffering the effect. 
 
MINOR DIVINE DISFAVOUR 
D8 
Effect 
1 
The character suffers 6 holy damage. 
2 
The character suffers 3 holy damage. 
3 
The character gains 2d6 drained conditions. 
4 
The character gains 1d6 drained conditions. 
5 
The character forgets the invocation for one week. 
6 
The character forgets the invocation for 1d6 days. 
7 
The character gains 2d6 stunned conditions. 
8 
The character gains 1d6 stunned conditions. 
 
MAJOR DIVINE DISFAVOUR 
D8 
Effect 
1 
The character suffers 12 holy damage, rend (1). 
2 
The character suffers 8 holy damage, rend (1). 
3 
The character suffers 6 holy damage. 
4 
The character suffers 3 holy damage. 
5 
The character gains 2d6 drained conditions. 
6 
The character gains 1d6 drained conditions. 
7 
The character forgets the invocation for one week. 
8 
The character forgets the invocation for 1d6 days. 
 
Blessings and Bless Points 
A character may utter words of supplication to their god, making a 
pray test. If the purpose of this pray test is not to invoke a prayer 
or miracle, then it may be used to generate bless points. Bless 
points are the culminated deific energy in an area towards a 
specific god. Bless points act as a currency that the players can 
spend on certain actions listed below. At the end of 1 turn (10 
minutes) all culminated bless points dissipate. The amount of 
bless points a character generates each time they use an action to 
test pray is directly related to their current proficiency level in the 
pray skill. This is seen on the table below: 
 
BLESS POINTS GENERATED  
Pray Prof Level 
Bless Points Generated 
Untrained 
1 
Trained 
2 
Journeyman 
2 
Expert 
3 
Master 
3 
Grandmaster 
4 
 
Spending Bless Points 
Anyone in the player’s group may spend bless points at any time 
(and in theory, foes of the party may also have generated their own 
separate pool of bless points which can be spent). 
 
Below is a list of things that bless points may be spent on: 
▶ Reroll Failed Test (1 Point): The character may reroll a failed 
test. This may not be done to critical failures. 
▶ +3 Bonus Test (1 Point): The character may add +3 to a failed 
test if it will make it a success. This may not be done to critical 
failures. 
▶ Remove Damage (2 Points): When the character is subject to 
an effect or attack that causes damage, they may immediately 
spend 2 bless points to reduce the damage taken by 2. 
▶ Stabilise (2 Points): When a character is dying, they may 
immediately spend 2 bless points to stabilise and remove all 
bleeding conditions. 
▶ Extra Move/Action (3 Points): The character may take an extra 
move and action immediately. 
▶ Seize Initiative (3 Points): The character’s side automatically 
gains the initiative on the next combat round. 


Invocations and Blessings 
65 
 
Prayer Descriptions 
Prayer of Battle 
Duration: 1 turn (10 minutes) 
Range: 60’ 
Invoking: 
▶ Critical Failure: Minor Disfavour, Fails. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The priest chooses a creature within range. That creature 
gains a +3 bonus to all attack tests for the duration. 
▶ Critical Success: The priest chooses up to three creatures within 
range. These creatures gain a +3 bonus to all attack tests for the 
duration. 
 
Prayer of Bravery 
Duration: 1 turn (10 minutes) 
Range: 60’ 
Invoking: 
▶ Critical Failure: Minor Disfavour, Fails. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The priest chooses a creature within range. That creature 
gains a +3 bonus to all Composure tests for the duration. In 
addition, for the duration, the affected creature is immune to 
frightened conditions. 
▶ Critical Success: The priest chooses up to three creatures within 
range. These creatures gain a +3 bonus to all Composure tests for 
the duration. In addition, for the duration, the affected creatures are 
immune to frightened conditions. 
 
Prayer of Clarity 
Duration: 1 turn (10 minutes) 
Range: 60’ 
Invoking: 
▶ Critical Failure: Minor Disfavour, Fails. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The priest chooses a creature within range. That creature 
gains a +3 bonus to all Lore tests for the duration, so long as they 
are made for Lore specialism in which they are at least Trained.  
▶ Critical Success: The priest chooses up to three creatures within 
range. These creatures gain a +3 bonus to all Lore tests for the 
duration, so long as they are made for Lore specialism in which they 
are at least Trained.  
 
Prayer of Defence 
Duration: 1 turn (10 minutes) 
Range: 60’ 
Invoking: 
▶ Critical Failure: Minor Disfavour, Fails. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The priest chooses a creature within range. That creature 
gains a 6+ power save on all of their hit locations for the duration. 
▶ Critical Success: The priest chooses up to three creatures within 
range. These creatures gain a 6+ power save on all of their hit 
locations for the duration. 
 
Prayer of Luck 
Duration: 1 turn (10 minutes) 
Range: 60’ 
Invoking: 
▶ Critical Failure: Minor Disfavour, Fails. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The priest chooses a creature within range. That creature 
may reroll all natural ‘1’s and ‘2’s rolled as part of a test. They must 
accept the second result. 
▶ Critical Success: The priest chooses up to three creatures within 
range. These creatures may reroll all natural ‘1’s and ‘2’s rolled as 
part of a test. They must accept the second result. 
 
Prayer of Malediction 
Duration: 1 turn (10 minutes) 
Range: 60’ 
Invoking: 
▶ Critical Failure: Minor Disfavour, Fails. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The priest chooses a creature within range. That creature 
suffers a -3 penalty to all attack tests for the duration. 
▶ Critical Success: The priest chooses up to three creatures within 
range. These creatures suffer a -3 penalty to all attack tests for the 
duration. 
 
Prayer of Protection Against Undead 
Duration: 1 turn (10 minutes) 
Range: 60’ 
Invoking: 
▶ Critical Failure: Minor Disfavour, Fails. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The priest chooses a creature within range. Any undead 
creature that attempts to attack the target must save vs PER before 
it does so. On a failure, the undead creature cannot make the attack. 
▶ Critical Success: The priest chooses up to three creatures within 
range. Any undead creature that attempts to attack the targets must 
save vs PER before it does so. On a failure, the undead creature 
cannot make the attack. 
 
 
 
Prayer of Relief 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 60’ 
Invoking: 
▶ Critical Failure: Minor Disfavour, Fails. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The priest chooses a creature within range. That creature 
instantly removes up to four bleeding conditions. 
▶ Critical Success: The priest chooses a creature within range. That 
creature instantly removes all its bleeding conditions. 
 
Prayer of Repose 
Duration: Variable (See Below) 
Range: Touch 
Invoking: 
▶ Critical Failure: Minor Disfavour, Fails. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The priest touches a dead body. For 3d6 days the body 
cannot be resurrected as an undead creature, and the corpse does 
not show signs of any decay. 
▶ Critical Success: The priest touches a dead body. For 6d6 days 
the body cannot be resurrected as an undead creature, and the 
corpse does not show signs of any decay. 
 
 


Invocations and Blessings 
66 
 
Prayer of Relief 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 60’ 
Invoking: 
▶ Critical Failure: Minor Disfavour, Fails. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The priest chooses a creature within range. That creature 
gains an additional 1HP after their next extended rest as part of 
natural healing. 
▶ Critical Success: The priest chooses up to three creatures within 
range. These creatures gain an additional 1HP after their next 
extended rest as part of natural healing. 
 
Prayer of Retreat 
Duration: 1 turn (10 minutes) 
Range: 60’ 
Invoking: 
▶ Critical Failure: Minor Disfavour, Fails. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The priest chooses a creature within range. That creature 
does not provoke attacks of opportunity for the duration. 
▶ Critical Success: The priest chooses up to three creatures within 
range. These creatures do not provoke attacks of opportunity for the 
duration. 
 
Prayer of Virtue 
Duration: 1 turn (10 minutes) 
Range: 60’ 
Invoking: 
▶ Critical Failure: Minor Disfavour, Fails. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The priest chooses a creature within range. That 
creature’s weapons count as magical for the duration. 
▶ Critical Success: The priest chooses up to three creatures within 
range. These creatures’ weapons count as magical for the duration. 
 
Miracle Descriptions 
Below a player will find all the miracles of each type of god. Game 
masters are encouraged to tailor these groups of miracles to the 
gods of their particular campaign worlds. Remember, a character 
may only ever learn the miracles of one particular god.  
Generic God Miracles 
These miracles are for gods with no specific milieu and may be 
used by characters who wish to dedicate themselves to the 
veneration of a monotheistic deity. 
 
Exorcise (5FP Cost) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 60’ 
Invoking: 
▶ Critical Failure: Major Disfavour, Fails. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: All undead type creatures within 30’ of the priest gain 2 
burning conditions. If they are invisible, then the exorcism renders 
them visible by limning them in white fire. 
▶ Critical Success: All undead type creatures within 30’ of the priest 
gain 4 burning conditions. If they are invisible, then the exorcism 
renders them visible by limning them in white fire. 
 
Fiery Purification (5FP Cost) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 60’ 
Invoking: 
▶ Critical Failure: Major Disfavour, Fails. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The priest chooses up to three targets within range that 
must save vs PER. On a failure, the target gains 4 burning 
conditions. On a success, they gain 2 burning conditions. 
▶ Critical Success: The priest chooses up to three targets within 
range. Each the target gains 4 burning conditions.  
 
 


Invocations and Blessings 
 
67 
 
Healing Benediction (6FP Cost) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: Touch 
Invoking: 
▶ Critical Failure: Major Disfavour, Fails. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The priest touches a willing creature, restoring 3 hit 
points. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster touches a willing creature, restoring 
6 hit points. 
 
 
Holy Vestments (4FP Cost) 
Duration: 1 turn (10 minutes) 
Range: Self 
Invoking: 
▶ Critical Failure: Major Disfavour, Fails. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: For the duration, the priest gains a 6+ power save on 
all of their own hit locations. 
▶ Critical Success: For the duration, the priest gains a 5+ power 
save on all of their own hit locations. 
 
 
Purifying Weapon (9FP Cost) 
Duration: 1 turn (10 minutes) 
Range: Touch 
Invoking: 
▶ Critical Failure: Major Disfavour, Fails. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The priest touches a weapon that then begins to glow 
with holy power. For the duration, attacks with the weapon deal an 
additional 3 damage per attack. All damage from the weapon gains 
the Rend (2) quality (or increases its Rend value by 2 if it already 
has Rend). In addition, the wielder of the weapon may freely change 
the damage of the weapon from its normal damage type to holy 
damage. For the duration, the weapon counts as magical. 
▶ Critical Success: The priest touches a weapon that then begins to 
glow with holy power. For the duration, attacks with the weapon 
deal an additional 5 damage per attack. All damage from the weapon 
gains the Rend (3) quality (or increases its Rend value by 3 if it 
already has Rend). In addition, the wielder of the weapon may freely 
change the damage of the weapon from its normal damage type to 
holy damage. For the duration, the weapon counts as magical.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Searing Smite (8FP Cost) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 120’ 
Invoking: 
▶ Critical Failure: Major Disfavour, Fails. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The priest chooses one target within range that suffers 
8 holy damage, rend (1) to a random hit location. 
▶ Critical Success: The priest chooses one target within range that 
suffers 12 holy damage, rend (2) to a random hit location.  
 
 
 


 
68 
 
Magic and Spells
Magic in Mud, Blood & Rats is dangerous. It is the stuff of grimy 
corruption and those who dabble in sorcery endanger their souls 
and risk the attention of demonic forces beyond this world. Despite 
this, some individuals develop a capacity for the arts and are, at least 
partially, able to control the fickle floes of supernatural power.  
 
Heritage Restriction of Magic 
Magick in the grubby fantasy genre has always been defined by 
ecstatic power and deep, unpredictable and immutable laws. To 
reinforce some of the traditional aspects of the genre game masters 
should use the following rules: 
 
▶ Dwarfs, Halflings and Ogres: These heritages cannot learn 
spells. Their physiology is hard-wired to resist the inimical nature 
of magic and therefore they cannot learn spells of any kind. 
Careers such as the wizard’s apprentice are generally unavailable to 
these heritages anyway, but this provides justification. 
▶ Humans: Human magicians may harness magical power, but the 
fragile, short-lived nature of humanity means that their bodies and 
souls struggle contain more than mere fragments of control. It is 
therefore suggested that humans only ever be permitted to learn 
minor magicks, black magic, diabolism, necromancy and one 
domain of other spells, such as the destruction domain and no 
others. This restriction is reflected in the talents and careers 
available to humans. 
▶ Elves: The lineage of an elf is tied strongly to that of the realm 
of magic. For this reason, elves suffer no restrictions when learning 
magic and may learn any type of spell from any group or domain. 
 
Black Magic, Diabolism and Necromancy 
There are three groups of spells that are not considered Minor 
Magick or a specific domain. These are the broad categories of 
Black Magic, Diabolism and Necromancy. There is no restriction on 
learning these spells in addition to other domains, but doing so is 
not without consequence. Each is corrosive to the soul and using 
such spells will almost certainly lead to madness and mutation if 
used persistently and players are warned against their liberal use. 
 
Sorcery Dice 
To cast a spell a character requires sorcery dice. These dice 
represent a character’s ability to summon and control the fickle 
energies of the arcane world. These energies may then be directed 
and channelled in ways that most common folk understand to be 
spells. The number of sorcery dice a character can use at once is 
based on their Sorcery skill proficiency level, as seen below: 
 
SORCERY DICE  
Sorcery Skill Proficiency Level 
Number of Sorcery Dice 
Untrained 
None 
Trained 
1d6 
Journeyman 
2d6 
Expert 
3d6 
Master 
4d6 
Grandmaster 
5d6 
 
When casting a spell, a character chooses how many of their sorcery 
dice to assign to a particular spell and then attempts to cast the spell 
using a power roll as their action in the Supernatural Abilities step 
in combat. Using a greater number of sorcery dice increases the 
chance of the spell being cast, but assigning more than required 
often runs the risk of deleterious effects if multiple natural ‘1’s are 
rolled. When these sorcery dice are rolled, each d6 counts as a 
‘success’ if a ‘5’ or more is rolled (this target number may be 
improved by certain talents). 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Casting Spells 
To cast a spell, a character follows the following steps: 
 
1. Declare Spell: The character declares which spell they will 
attempt to cast from the spells they know. To cast a spell a character 
must be able to move their hands and speak. 
 
2. Declare Sorcery Dice: The character decides how many of their 
sorcery dice they will use to attempt to cast the spell. 
 
3. Make Power Roll: The character rolls their declared sorcery dice 
and determines how many ‘successes’ they have scored. A roll of a 
‘5’ or more is required on a sorcery die to be counted as a ‘success’. 
 
4. Consult Spell Entry: Consult the spell’s entry – it will list how 
many successes were required to cast the spell. If the required 
number of successes were met or exceeded, the spell is cast. 
 
Reading Spell Entries 
When a spell is cast, whether successfully or unsuccessfully, consult 
the entry for the spell. If the number of successes generated by the 
power roll was equal or higher than the spell’s required successes, 
then the success entry takes effect. 
 
▶ ‘1’ and ‘6’ entries: Some spells will list additional effects if the 
sorcery dice used in the power roll were natural ‘1s’ and ‘6’s. The 
number of ‘1’ or ‘6’s required to trigger these additional effects will 
be listed, for example 1/1 or more means that this effect only 
triggers if two or more sorcery dice result in natural ‘1’s. Similarly, 
an entry of 6/6/6 or more would mean that this effect is only 
manifested if three or more of the sorcery dice were natural ‘6s’. 
 
Multiple Entries 
It is possible that a caster triggers multiple effects from a single 
spell. If this is the case, then all entries are triggered though it may 
be that some entries (usually natural ‘6’ entries) cancel the normal 
success entry by replacing it. 
 
Durations, Targets and Spheres 
▶ All spells will list their legitimate targets. Such ranges are given in 
feet or apply only to the priest themselves. 
▶ All spells will list durations; this will be given explicitly but may 
vary depending on the level of the caster. 
 
Spellcasting Damage Modifier 
Some people are better at casting spells than others, similar to how 
other individuals are more proficient at wielding a sword or 
shooting a gun. In Mud, Blood & Rats, a creature that casts a spell 
which causes damage gains a bonus to their damage based on their 
Intellect proficiency level, as seen in the table below. This bonus 
is known as the Spellcasting Damage Bonus. In spell entries this 
is often abbreviated to SDB.  
 
SPELLCASTING DAMAGE BONUS 
INT 
Bonus Damage to Spells 
Untrained 
- 
Trained 
+1 
Journeyman 
+1 
Expert 
+2 
Master 
+2 
Grandmaster 
+3 
 
 


Magic and Spells 
69 
 
Domains 
Creation 
This is the domain of artifice. It is concerned with the fabrication 
of physical goods and as such is strongly tied to ideas regarding 
the transmutation of base material. 
Darkness (Available in the Complete Edition) 
This is the domain of shadows and nighttime. This domain grants 
its wielder access to spells of illusion and concealment as well as 
hidden escape. 
Death (Available in the Complete Edition) 
The domain of death is concerned with the relationship between 
the animated part of the self and its link to, but separation from, 
the soul. The death domain is not necromancy but is concerned 
with the preservation and respect for those who are dead or dying.  
Destruction (Available in the Complete Edition) 
Destruction is opposed by creation. Spells of this domain grant 
fulminating power to scorch, damage and obliterate elements of 
the physical world. It is strongly associated with fire and burning. 
Land (Available in the Complete Edition) 
The domain of the land is related to the natural world. It allows its 
practitioners to control beasts and commune with nature spirits 
such as those that inhabit rivers and woods. 
Life (Available in the Complete Edition) 
The is the magic of plants and herblore. It is tangentially related to 
the decay of the natural world. Practitioners of life magic can 
revitalise their allies and, in the most extreme cases, even restore 
the dead to life. 
Light (Available in the Complete Edition) 
Light magic is radiant power. It is suffused with both the 
illuminating power of light and its ability to sear, burn and purify 
the darkness which it hates. 
Sky (Available in the Complete Edition) 
Magic associated with the sky is the deep study of astrological 
movements and how they are expressions of the mortal action and 
the will of the gods. For the most part, sky magic is the art of 
divination. 
 
Magical Mishaps 
Sometimes a magician may fail to cast their spell. Most of the time, 
this will not result in anything worse than a bit of minor 
embarrassment. But occasionally something disastrous happens. 
Casting entries that result in Magical Mishaps cause the caster roll 
on the tables below. 
 
MINOR MAGICAL MISHAP TABLE 
2d6 
Effect 
2 
The caster immediately rolls on the major magical 
mishap table. 
3 
The caster summons a minor demon that appears 
within 30’. The caster gains a point of Grime and 1d6 
drained conditions. 
4

gains a point of grime and 1d6 drained conditions. 
5 
The caster is blasted with aetheric energy, suffering 6 
arcane damage, rend (2) which hits the body. 
6 
The caster’s clothes immediately catch fire due to the 
magical feedback. The caster gains 2 burning 
conditions. 
7 
The character is blinded for 1d6 rounds as visions of 
hell fill their senses. 
8 
The character gains 1d6 stunned conditions as their 
body is buffeted by energy. 
9 
The character vomits over themselves, gaining 2 
drained conditions. 
10 
Nauseating fumes billow from the caster. All creatures 
within 30’ of the caster must save vs END or gain 3 
drained conditions. 
11 
Sharp, stabbing needles of energy shoot from the caster. 
All creatures within 30’ of the caster suffer 2 piercing 
damage, rend (3) on a random hit location. 
12 
The caster loses 2HP as their body fills with energy. 
Next time their cast a spell they gain +3 additional 
sorcery dice.

The character’s soul is injured and bleeds into hell. The
