Counterspelling 
It is technically possible for a magician to dispel another wizard’s 
spell as it is cast. This is a highly dangerous affair which can easily 
backfire.  
 
A creature with at least 1 sorcery dice that can see a creature 
casting a spell may attempt to counterspell it. A successful 
counterspell means that the original spell that is being cast fails to 
manifest. 
 
When a creature attempts to counterspell they commit as many of 
their sorcery dice as they wish to the attempt. Every success they 
score on their sorcery dice cancels one success on the enemy’s 
sorcery dice power roll. However, the counterspelling character 
must cancel every enemy success this way or none of them are 
cancelled. Additionally, every natural ‘1’ that the counterspelling 
character rolls causes the counterspeller to lose 1HP. A character 
may only attempt to counterspell once per round. 
 
 
 
 
 
 


Magic and Spells 
70 
 
Minor Magicks 
Minor magick is a type of spell available to all practitioners of the 
sorcerous arts. It encompasses a wide range of spells that offer 
lesser magical effects. Most magicians and hedge witches start their 
arcane careers by learning several minor magickal spells. 
 
Arcane Arrow (1 Success) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 120’ 
A glittering dart, dripping with fog, arcs from the fingertips of the 
caster to strike down their enemy. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster loses 2HP. 
▶ Success: The caster throws a dart of magical energy at a foe within 
range. The dart automatically hits but a hit-location roll is still 
required. The dart causes 3+SDB arcane damage. 
▶ 6/6 or more: The caster throws three darts of magical energy at 
up to three different foes within range. The darts automatically hit 
but a hit-location roll is still required. The darts cause 3+SDB arcane 
damage each. This effect replaces the normal success effect. 
 
Blight (1 Success) 
Duration: 1 turn (10 minutes) 
Range: 120’ 
The caster’s veins throb as they summon a smoke-like serpent that 
slithers from their lungs to become a bane to their enemies. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster loses 1HP. 
▶ Success: The caster chooses one target within range. The target 
must save vs PER or suffer a -3 penalty to all tests made.  
▶ 6/6 or more: The caster chooses one target within range. The 
target must save vs PER or suffer a -6 penalty to all tests made. This 
effect replaces the normal success effect. 
 
Compass Conduit (1 Success) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: Self 
In the deep wilderness, it helps if a caster can manipulate the ley lines 
and learn their current location. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster loses 1HP. 
▶ Success: The caster instantly learns which way is north, what time 
of day it is and how far the nearest settlement is in miles. 
 
 
Dizzying Dart (1 Success) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 120’ 
A fuzzy, blue-green bolt of prickly energy leaps from the caster. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster loses 2HP. 
▶ Success: The caster throws a dart of scintillating light at a foe 
within range. The dart automatically hits. The target gains 3 stunned 
conditions. 
▶ 6/6 or more: The caster throws three darts of magical energy at 
up to three different foes within range. The darts automatically hit. 
The targets gain 3 stunned conditions each. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Drowse (1 Success) 
Duration: 1 turn/caster’s level 
Range: Touch 
The caster’s eyes become pools of green, relaxing light which invites 
a deep sleep. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster must immediately save vs PER or fall 
asleep for the duration. They may be woken naturally.  
▶ Success: The caster touches a creature of level 4 or lower, 
attempting to put them to sleep. The creature must immediately save 
vs PER or fall into a natural sleep for the duration. 
▶ 6/6 or more: The caster touches a creature of level 8 or lower, 
attempting to put them to sleep. The creature must immediately save 
vs PER or fall into a natural sleep for the duration. 
 
 
 
Enchant Beast (1 Success) 
Duration: Varies (see below) 
Range: 80’ 
The caster whispers to the moth, telling it to go fetch aid to the caster. 
▶ 1/1: The caster loses 2HP.  
▶ Success: The caster may enchant (see below) up to 1d6 levels’ 
worth of animals. Each creature affected may roll a save vs PER to 
resist. 
▶ 6/6: The caster may enchant (see below) up to 2d6 levels’ worth 
of animals. Each creature affected may roll a save vs PER to resist. 
This effect replaces the normal success effect. 
 
Enchanted: 
A creature that is enchanted treats the caster as a friend and ally. 
They are not under the caster’s direct command but will generally 
heed the caster’s requests as would a close friend. A creature retests 
its Save vs PER based on its Intellect proficiency level: 
▶ Untrained: Once per month. 
▶ Trained and Journeyman: Once per week. 
▶ Expert: Once per day. 
▶ Master: Once per hour. 
▶ Grandmaster: Once per turn (10 minutes). 
 
▶ Enchanted Creatures count as followers as per the rules for 
Personality. 
 
Feign Fatality (1 Success) 
Duration: 3d6 hours 
Range: 30’ 
The orcs search the pile of corpses, finishing off all who show signs of 
life. At this, the caster drops into a cold, clammy yet unreal death. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster loses 1HP. 
▶ Success: The caster drops into a death -like state for the duration 
of the spell. To the untrained eye they seem dead. A very hard (-6) 
Healing (First Aid) test must be made by a creature to determine 
the truth about the caster. While in this state the caster is not aware 
of their surroundings and cannot be awoken until the duration of 
the spell is completed. 
 
Glow (1 Success) 
Duration: 1 turn/caster’s level 
Range: Touch 
The caster touches their staff’s tip, which instantly blooms in a 
glowing light. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster loses 1HP. 
▶ Success: The caster touches an object which then radiates a warm 
light up to 30’ from itself. 
 
 


Magic and Spells 
71 
 
Phantom Sounds (1 Success) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 120’ 
The guards are momentarily distracted as it sounds like a gate 
behind them has just slammed shut.  
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster loses 1HP. 
▶ Success: The caster chooses one point within range. A sound is 
emitted from that point. The sound cannot be words but can 
otherwise be natural noises such as something falling over, the 
sound of a splash or the moving of footsteps.  
 
Protection from Rain (1 Success) 
Duration: 1 hour/caster’s level 
Range: Self 
While their allies trudge on through the soaking rain, the same 
droplets bounce harmlessly away from the caster. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster loses 1HP. 
▶ Success: For the duration the caster is protected from the heaviest 
rain, which lands roughly an inch from them and then slides off as 
though an invisible force stops it from touching the caster. 
 
Projectile Protection (1 Success) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: Self 
The musket ball bounces from the caster’s robe as though it had 
struck a stone wall. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster loses 2HP. 
▶ Success: The caster gains a 6+ power save on all their hit 
locations but only against attacks made by missile weapons. 
 
Purify Putrescence (1 Success) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 30’ 
With a movement of their hand the caster restores the hunk of rotting 
meat to a state fit for eating. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster loses 1HP.  
▶ Success: The caster waves their hands over a meal’s worth of food 
that may have spoiled, or water that is brackish and undrinkable. 
Instantly the item becomes fresh and safe to consume.  
 
Restorative Touch (1 Success) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: Touch 
The caster places their hands upon their ally, uttering incantations 
as the target’s bloody wounds coagulate and their breathing calms. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster loses 2HP. 
▶ Success: The caster touches a creature and then spends 2FP. The 
touched creature instantly removes 2 bleeding conditions or regains 
2HP. If the caster successfully casts the spell but does not have 2FP 
to spend (or refuses to spend it) then the spell has no effect. 
▶ 6/6 or more: The caster touches a creature and then spends 4FP. 
The touched creature instantly removes 4 bleeding conditions or 
regains 4HP. If the caster successfully casts the spell but does not 
have 4FP to spend (or refuses to spend it) then the spell has no 
effect. This effect replaces the normal success effect. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Sanguine Sigil (1 Success) 
Duration: 1 turn (10 minutes) 
Range: 120’ 
The caster touches the weapon, which glows red with smouldering, 
angry runes. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster loses 1HP. 
▶ Success: The caster touches one weapon. For the duration of the 
spell, that weapon deals +1 additional damage.  
▶ 6/6 or more: The caster touches one weapon. For the duration of 
the spell, that weapon deals +2 additional damage. This effect 
replaces the normal success effect. 
 
Spectral Shudder (1 Success) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 120’ 
The door of the tower slams shut suddenly, sending a thrill of 
uncertain horror up the spines of the interlopers. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster loses 1HP. 
▶ Success: The caster chooses one object of up to large size within 
range. That object shudders or moves unnaturally. This may be a 
door closing suddenly, a chest opening, a piece of crockery falling 
or other similar minor effect. 
 
Witch Glimmer (1 Success) 
Duration: 1 round/caster’s level 
Range: 120’ 
With a slight movement of the fingers, the enemy becomes limned in 
glowing light, making their attempted ambush impossible. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster loses 2HP.  
▶ Success: The caster chooses one creature in range that they can 
see. That creature makes a save vs PER. On a failure they become 
limned in harmless technicolour fire for the duration. Due to the 
nature of these flames, all attack tests against the target are made 
with a +3 bonus for the duration. In addition, the target suffers a -6 
to all Stealth tests for the duration. 
▶ 6/6 or more: The caster chooses up to three creatures in range 
that they can see. The targets make a save vs PER. On a failure they 
become limned in harmless technicolour fire for the duration. Due 
to the nature of these flames, all attack tests against the targets are 
made with a +3 bonus for the duration. In addition, the targets suffer 
a -6 to all Stealth tests for the duration. This effect replaces the 
normal success effect. 
 


Magic and Spells 
72 
 
Domain: Creation 
The creation domain is concerned with the manipulation of artifice. 
Despite its name, the creation domain is one of the most unnatural 
forms of magic as it is grounded in the control of constructs and 
pertains to actions such as transmutation. Practitioners of this 
domain are often referred to as transmuters or artificers. 
 
Alter Artifice (2 Successes) 
Duration: Permanent 
Range: Touch 
The caster grabs their foe’s sword watching it droop pathetically, 
rendering it unusable. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster rolls on the minor magical mishap table. 
▶ Success: The caster touches one object made of either metal or 
wood and may, for 60 seconds, mould it like clay, after which is 
rehardens.  
▶ 6/6/6 or more: The caster touches any number of objects made 
of either metal or wood and may, for 10 minutes, mould them like 
clay, after which they reharden. This effect replaces the normal 
success effect. 
 
Fool’s Gold (2 Successes) 
Duration: 1 hour 
Range: Touch 
The wizard grins as they hand over the rare spice to the eager-eyed 
dwarfs. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster rolls on the minor magical mishap table. 
▶ Success: The caster touches up to 6 pfennigs or shillings. For the 
duration they are transmuted to gold. After the spell duration is 
ended, they revert to their original material. 
▶ 6/6/6 or more: The caster touches up to 6 pfennigs or shillings. 
For the duration they are transmuted to gold. After the spell duration 
is ended, they revert to their original material. The spell’s duration 
is now one week, not 1 hour as listed above. This effect replaces the 
normal success effect. 
Scintillating Shroud (2 Successes) 
Duration: 1 turn (10 minutes) 
Range: Self 
The caster enwraps themselves in a glittering robe of silver-gold light. 
As their foes bear down upon them the cloak shimmers with 
brilliance, protecting the wizard. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster rolls on the minor magical mishap table. 
▶ Success: The caster’s clothing becomes a blinding silver-gold 
colour. Any creature that attempts an attack test against the caster 
for the duration of this spell must save vs END or gain a blinded 
condition that lasts 1 turn. 
▶ 6/6/6 or more: The caster’s clothing becomes a blinding silver-
gold colour. Any creature that attempts an attack test against the 
caster for the duration of this spell must save vs END or gain a 
blinded condition that lasts 1 turn and 6 stunned conditions. This 
effect replaces the normal success effect. 
 
Widoghast’s Whetstone (2 Successes) 
Duration: 1 turn (10 minutes) 
Range: 15’ 
The caster takes the sword of their ally and begins to caress it. As they 
slowly sing to the blade, it becomes imbued with power from the plane 
of artifice, growing strong by the second. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster rolls on the minor magical mishap table. 
▶ Success: The caster chooses one melee weapon within range. 
That weapon counts as magical and gains the rend (1) quality. If the 
weapon already has the rend quality, then it is improved by 1. 
▶ 6/6/6 or more: The caster chooses up to three melee weapons 
within range. These weapons count as magical and gain the rend (2) 
quality. If the weapons already have the rend quality, then it is 
improved by 1. This effect replaces the normal success effect. 
 
 
 
 
 


Magic and Spells 
73 
 
Cloud of Corrosion (3 Successes) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 120’ 
The caster summons a billowing orange-silver cloud of tiny metallic 
insects. Wherever the insects land, any metal there rusts instantly. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster rolls on the minor magical mishap table. 
▶ Success: The caster summons a 20’ radius cloud at a point within 
range. Any non-precious metal within the cloud (such as iron or 
steel) instantly begins to rust. Attacks made with such weapons deal 
1 point fewer damage and armour saves suffer a -1 penalty for 
afflicted armour. If this reduces the armour save provided to 6+ or 
the weapon damage to 0, then the affected piece of equipment is 
destroyed. 
▶ 6/6/6/6 or more: The caster summons a 20’ radius cloud at a 
point within range. Any non-precious metal within the cloud (such 
as iron or steel) instantly rusts into flakes – it is totally destroyed. 
This effect replaces the normal success effect. 
 
Grapeshot (3 Successes) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 15’ cone 
From the palm of the caster’s hand comes a searing hot blast of super-
heated scrap, nails and rusted musket balls. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster rolls on the minor magical mishap table. 
▶ Success: The caster emits a 15’ cone of shrapnel. All creatures in 
the cone suffer 6+SDB piercing damage, rend (1). A hit location roll 
is not made – this damage is made against the body of the creature. 
▶ 6/6/6/6 or more: The caster emits a 25’ cone of shrapnel. All 
creatures in the cone suffer 10+SDB piercing damage, rend (1). This 
effect replaces the normal success effect. A hit location roll is not 
made – this damage is made against the body of the creature. This 
effect replaces the normal success effect. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Peal of Puzzlement (3 Successes) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 120’ 
The harsh ringing of bells fills the air as power from the domain of 
artifice floods the victim’s ears. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster rolls on the minor magical mishap table. 
▶ Success: The caster chooses a point within range. All creatures 
within a 10’ radius of the point instantly gain 5 stunned conditions. 
▶ 6/6/6/6 or more: The caster chooses a point within range. All 
enemy creatures within a 20’ radius of the point instantly gain 5 
stunned conditions. Creatures allied with the caster are unaffected. 
This effect replaces the normal success effect. 
 
 
 
 
 
Yarnoch’s Yawning Entry (3 Successes) 
Duration: 1 round/caster’s level 
Range: Touch 
The wall of the castle opens with a yawn, revealing a passageway. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster rolls on the minor magical mishap table. 
▶ Success: The caster creates a hole in a manufactured wall that is 
six feet in diameter. The hole is magically maintained and acts like 
a portal from one side of the wall to the other. When the spell ends, 
the wall is undamaged, returning to its original shape. The wall must 
have been manufactured, such as a timber, wattle and daub or stone 
wall and it must be no thicker than ten feet. 
▶ 6/6/6/6 or more: The caster creates a hole in a manufactured 
wall that is ten feet in diameter. The hole is magically maintained 
and acts like a portal from one side of the wall to the other. when 
the spell ends, the wall is undamaged, returning to its original shape. 
The wall must have been manufactured, such as a timber, wattle and 
daub or stone wall and it must be no thicker than twenty feet. This 
effect replaces the normal success effect. 
 
Canto’s Cannonball (4 Successes) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 15’ cone 
A furnace-hot cannonball streaks from the caster’s fingertips, 
gaining heat and lethality as it moves through the air. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1/1 or more: The caster rolls on the major magical mishap 
table. 
▶ Success: The caster chooses one target within range. The target 
instantly suffers 12+SDB piercing damage, rend (2), penetrate (20’). 
A hit location roll is required for this damage. 
▶ 6/6/6/6/6 or more: The caster chooses one target within range. 
The target makes a save vs AGI, instantly suffers 16+SDB piercing 
damage, rend (2), penetrate (20’). A hit location roll is required for 
this damage. This effect replaces the normal success effect. 
 
 


Magic and Spells 
74 
 
Fabricate (4 Successes) 
Duration: Permanent 
Range: 30’ 
The caster places their hands on the shattered door and their 
companions watch in rapture as a door grows and fits itself back 
into the frame perfectly. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1/1 or more: The caster rolls on the major magical mishap 
table. 
▶ Success: The caster spends 2FP and magically creates one item 
out of an organic material such as wood. The item is no larger than 
5 cubic feet and may not contain precious metals or gems. If the 
caster cannot spend 2FP, then the spell automatically fails to cast. 
6/6/6/6/6 or more: The caster spends 2FP and magically creates 
one item out of an organic material such as wood. The item is no 
larger than 50 cubic feet and may not contain precious metals or 
gems. If the caster cannot spend 2FP, then the spell automatically 
fails to cast. This effect replaces the normal success effect. 
 
Iron Curtain (4 Successes) 
Duration: 1 turn/caster’s level 
Range: 120’ 
As the cloud of arrows begins to reign down on the army, the artificer 
throws up a great wall of steaming iron. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1/1 or more: The caster rolls on the major magical mishap 
table. 
▶ Success: A wall of iron (see below) appears up to 60 feet long. 
6/6/6/6/6 or more: A wall of iron (see below) appears up to 200 
feet long. This effect replaces the normal success effect. 
 
Wall of Iron: 
▶ Size: The wall can be any size and can be shaped by the caster 
including straight walls or protective circles, the dimensions cannot 
exceed the length listed in the roll. The wall is 30’ high and 5’ wide. 
 
Midas’ Touch (5 Successes) 
Duration: Permanent 
Range: Touch 
The caster touches the charging orc which is instantly transformed 
into a glittering statue. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1/1 or more: The caster rolls on the major magical mishap 
table. 
▶ Success: The caster touches a creature that is half of their own 
level or lower. The target must save vs PER or be immediately 
transformed unto pyrite. 
▶ 6/6/6/6/6 or more: The caster touches a creature that is half of 
their own level or lower. The target must save vs PER or be 
immediately transformed into gold. The caster then gains a grime 
point. This effect replaces the normal success effect. 
 
Storm of Shrapnel (5 Successes) 
Duration: 1 round/caster’s level 
Range: 120’ 
The magician summons up a howling storm of heated metal that 
eviscerates everything in its path. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1/1 or more: The caster rolls on the major magical mishap 
table. 
▶ Success: The caster summons a 20’ radius storm of howling 
shrapnel at a point within range. Any creature beginning their turn 
in the storm or entering the storm once it has been summoned 
instantly suffers 8+SDB slashing damage, rend (1) and gains 2 
bleeding conditions. A hit location roll is not made – this damage is 
made against the body of the creature. 
▶ 6/6/6/6/6 or more: The caster summons a 30’ radius storm of 
howling shrapnel at a point within range. Any creature beginning 
their turn in the storm or entering the storm once it has been 
summoned instantly suffers 12+SDB slashing damage, rend (2) and 
gains 4 bleeding conditions. A hit location roll is not made – this 
damage is made against the body of the creature. This effect replaces 
the normal success effect.
 


 
75 
 
Black Magic 
Black magic, sometimes called dark magic, is the spellcraft of 
unlicenced wizards which are known as witches or warlocks by the 
common folk. It is a mixture of magical types which are not 
carefully purified by its practitioners (often because they have not 
been taught how to) which leaves traces of corruption in its 
incantation. 
 
 
 
Embrace Nature (2 Successes) 
Duration: 1 round/caster’s level 
Range: Self 
The caster leans into the ferns and mosses, becoming one with them. 
Casting: 
▶ 1 or more: The caster gains a grime point and loses 2HP. 
▶ Success: The caster touches one natural object of medium size or 
larger, such as a tree, boulder, pond, etc. They, along with all their 
carried possessions, instantly merge with the object. While in this 
form they cannot see, move or take any action, though they can still 
hear their surroundings. 
▶ 6/6/6 or more: The caster and up to six allies within 30’ touch 
one natural object of medium size or larger, such as a tree, boulder, 
pond, etc. They, along with all their carried possessions, instantly 
merge with the object. While in this form they cannot see, move or 
take any action, though they can still hear their surroundings. This 
effect replaces the normal success effect. 
 
Pricking Thumbs (2 Successes) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: Self 
The caster enters a trance. The nearby presence of a secret witches 
sabbath is revealed through a painful prickling of the caster’s 
thumbs. 
Casting: 
▶ 1 or more: The caster gains a grime point and loses 2HP. 
▶ Success: The caster attunes themselves temporarily to the forces 
of evil and corruption. They may instantly sense the presence of 
creatures that harbour malign or antagonistic intentions towards the 
caster within 60’. They also detect the presence of any object or 
creature that is a source of grime points. 
 
Sepulchral Bolt (2 Successes) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 120’ 
The caster flicks a glittering, hissing dart of black energy towards 
their foe. 
Casting: 
▶ 1 or more: The caster gains a grime point and loses 2HP. 
▶ Success: The caster throws a dart of magical energy at a foe within 
range. The dart automatically hits but a hit-location roll is still 
required. The dart causes 6+SDB necrotic damage, rend (1). 
▶ 6/6/6 or more: The caster throws three darts of magical energy 
at up to three different foes within range. The darts automatically hit 
but a hit-location roll is still required. The darts cause 6+SDB necrotic 
damage each. This effect replaces the normal success effect. 
 
Whispering Grimalkin (3 Successes) 
Duration: Varies (See Below) 
Range: 120’ 
The caster lifts a toad, whispering their schemes and plots into its 
ever-eager ears. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster gains a grime point and loses 2HP. 
▶ Success: The caster gains a grime point. The caster then speaks a 
short message to a small animal such as a black cat or a toad in a 
language that the animal automatically understands. This animal may 
then deliver this message to another creature within 100 miles. 
▶ 6/6/6 or more: As success but the animal gains the ability to fly 
at its normal walking speed (if it cannot already fly) and turn 
invisible until the message is delivered. 
 


 
76 
 
Witch’s Tickle (3 Successes) 
Duration: Varies (See Below) 
Range: 120’ 
The victim’s skin crawls with prickling pain that feels like the biting 
of many insects. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster gains a grime point and loses 2HP. 
▶ Success: The caster chooses one target within range. The target 
must be humanoid. The effects of witch’s tickle are varied depending 
on the hit location, which is determined now: 
Head: The target is blinded for 1d4+1 rounds. They must 
also make a hard (-3) save vs END or suffer 4+SDB piercing damage 
to this location that ignores armour saves. 
Body: The target gains 1d6 stunned conditions. They 
must also make a hard (-3) save vs END or suffer 4+SDB piercing 
damage to this location that ignores armour saves. 
Legs: The target falls prone and gains 2 stunned 
conditions. They must also make a hard (-3) save vs END or suffer 
4+SDB piercing damage to this location that ignores armour saves. 
▶ 6/6/6 or more: As success but the piercing damage caused is 
increased to 8. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Baleful Domination (4 Successes) 
Duration: Varies (see below) 
Range: 80’ 
The withered crone utters a word, and the knight turns to his 
companions and attempts to kill them. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1/1 or more: The caster gains 2 grime points and must roll on 
the minor magical mishap table. 
▶ Success: The caster gains 1 grime point and may control (see 
below) up to 3d6 levels’ worth of humanoids. Each creature affected 
may roll a save vs PER to resist. 
▶ 6/6/6/6: The caster may control (see below) up to 6d6 levels’ 
worth of humanoids. Each creature affected may roll a save vs PER 
to resist. Creatures of 3rd level or less do not get a save. This effect 
replaces the normal success effect. 
 
Control: 
A creature that is controlled is slavishly under the command of the 
caster, completing any action that the caster decrees to the best of 
their ability, including suicidal and dangerous commands. A creature 
retests its save vs PER based on its Intellect proficiency level: 
▶ Untrained: Once per week. 
▶ Trained and Journeyman: Once per day. 
▶ Expert: Once per hour. 
▶ Master: Once per turn (10 minutes). 
▶ Grandmaster: Once per round. 
 
▶ Controlled Creatures count as followers as per the rules for 
Personality. 
 
Cure of Thorns (4 Successes) 
Duration: Permanent 
Range: Self 
The caster moves their hands over their friend, causing wracking 
pain and gasping screams. Through the gurgling horror of the spell, 
the target’s missing hand begins to regrow. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1/1 or more: The caster gains a grime point and loses 2HP. 
▶ Success: The caster touches a target who is suffering from an 
unwanted physical ailment or who has a missing body part such as 
an arm or eye. The target suffers 3d6 slashing damage which ignores 
armour saves. If the target is reduced to 0HP they are dying – roll 
on the critical hit table with the excess damage as usual and count 
the location as a body hit. If the character survives, then the physical 
ailment that was troubling them is cured, or the missing body part 
is regrown. 
 
Wilt and Wither (5 Successes) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 120’ 
With a flick of the wrist, the crone laughs as their victim doubles over 
and then begins to spasm with pain as blistering energy snakes 
through their anatomy. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1/1/1 or more: The caster gains a grime point and loses 2HP. 
▶ Success: The caster chooses a foe within range. The target suffers 
14+SDB necrotic damage, but a hit-location roll is still required.  
▶ 6/6/6/6/6/6 or more: The target suffers 24 necrotic damage, but 
a hit-location roll is still required. This effect replaces the normal 
success effect. 
 


 
77 
 
Diabolism 
Diabolism is a dangerous, fickle art concerned with the conjuration 
and control of demons. It is highly illegal, dangerous and a 
profound imperilment to anyone who engages with it.  
 
Infernal Messenger (2 Successes) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: Touch 
Touching the target, the warlock draws away their essence, adding it 
to their own. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster rolls on the minor magical mishap table. 
▶ Success: The caster summons a small demon. The demon’s sole 
purpose on this plane is to deliver a message of the caster’s choosing 
to a person they name. The message may not be longer than 25 
words long. The demon stays on this plane until exorcised or until 
it has delivered its message. It has a fly speed of 30’ per round.  
▶ 6/6/6 or more: The caster summons a small demon. The demon’s 
sole purpose on this plane is to deliver a message of the caster’s 
choosing to a person they name. The message may not be longer 
than 50 words long. The demon stays on this plane until exorcised 
or until it has delivered its message. It has a fly speed of 50’ per 
round. This effect replaces the normal success effect. 
 
Summon Minor Demon (3 Successes) 
Duration: Permanent 
Range: 30’ 
A cauldron bubbles and froths just moments before its murky surface 
bursts with teeming demonic maggot-things. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster rolls on the minor magical mishap table. 
▶ Success: The caster summons a single minor demon within 
range. The demon slavishly follows the caster’s command but does 
not count as one of the caster’s followers as determined by the rules 
for Personality. The caster gains a grime point. 
▶ 6/6/6/6 or more: The caster summons a 1d6 minor demons 
within range. The demons slavishly follow the caster’s command but 
do not count as one of the caster’s followers as determined by the 
rules for Personality. The caster gains a grime point. This effect 
replaces the normal success effect. 
 
Summon Major Demon (4 Successes) 
Duration: Permanent 
Range: 30’ 
With a blade of burning runes and armour of black-iron and bound 
screams, the red-skinned demon steps from the wound in the world 
to serve the caster. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1/1 or more The caster rolls on the major magical mishap 
table. 
▶ Success: The caster summons a single demon of covetousness, 
deceit, despair or wrath within range. The demon slavishly follows 
the caster’s command but does not count as one of the caster’s 
followers as determined by the rules for Personality. The caster 
gains 2 grime points. 
▶ 6/6/6/6/6 or more: The caster summons a 1d6 demon of 
covetousness, deceit, despair or wrath within range. The demons 
slavishly follow the caster’s command but do not count as one of the 
caster’s followers as determined by the rules for Personality. The 
caster gains 2 grime points. This effect replaces the normal success 
effect. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Consultation of Damnation (5 Successes) 
Duration: Permanent 
Range: Self 
Rising from the garden of despair and delight, the roiling form of the 
demon finds its attention drawn to the mortal world, to convey 
forbidden knowledge. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1/1/1 or more The caster rolls on the major magical mishap 
table. 
▶ Success: The caster makes magical contact with a hell prince. 
The demon will generally be irritated at the communication but is 
bound to answer the caster three questions that it is asked. It must 
answer truthfully. It always knows the answer, except in the most 
extreme circumstances. The caster gains 2 grime points. 
▶ 6/6/6/6/6 or more: The caster makes magical contact with a hell 
prince. The demon will generally be irritated at the communication 
but is bound to answer the caster three questions that it is asked. It 
must answer truthfully. It always knows the answer, except in the 
most extreme circumstances. This effect replaces the normal success 
effect. 
 
 
 


 
78 
 
Necromancy 
Necromancy is the manipulation and control of the dead. It is a 
most heinous act that is uniformly condemned and hated by the 
witch hunters and the common folk alike. 
 
Grave Touch (2 Successes) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: Touch 
Touching the target, the warlock draws away their essence, adding it 
to their own. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster rolls on the minor magical mishap table. 
▶ Success: The caster touches a target creature which instantly loses 
3HP. The caster then gains 3HP. 
▶ 6/6/6 or more: The caster touches a target creature which 
instantly loses 4HP. The caster then gains 4HP.This effect replaces 
the normal success effect. 
 
Animate Corpse (3 Successes) 
Duration: Permanent 
Range: Touch 
With a raise of their outstretched hands, the corpses of the caster’s 
enemies rise to do their master’s bidding in death. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1 or more: The caster rolls on the minor magical mishap table. 
▶ Success: The caster touches a humanoid corpse and animates it 
as an undead skeleton or zombie. The skeleton or zombie slavishly 
follows the caster’s command but does not count as one of the 
caster’s followers as determined by the rules for Personality. The 
caster gains a grime point. 
▶ 6/6/6/6 or more: The caster creates touches up to six humanoid 
corpses and reanimates them as undead skeletons or zombies. The 
creatures slavishly follow the caster’s command but does not count 
as one of the caster’s followers as determined by the rules for 
Personality. The caster gains a grime point. This effect replaces the 
normal success effect. 
 
Steal Youth (4 Successes) 
Duration: Permanent 
Range: Touch 
The necromancer cackles in delight as they drain the youth from the 
young peasant girl, reducing her to a grey-haired crone before her 
time. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1/1 or more The caster rolls on the major magical mishap 
table. 
▶ Success: The caster touches the target and drains 2d10 years of 
their life, transferring it to the caster, making them that many years 
younger again. This may kill the target creature. The caster gains 2 
grime points. 
▶ 6/6/6/6/6 or more: The caster touches the target and drains 
2d10+20 years of their life, transferring it to the caster, making them 
that many years younger again. This may kill the target creature. The 
caster gains 2 grime points. This effect replaces the normal success 
effect. 
 
 
Army of Carrion (5 Successes) 
Duration: Permanent 
Range: Variable (see below) 
When the chanting stops the cemetery explodes as corpses, old and 
new, rise to meet the call of the necromancer. 
Casting: 
▶ 1/1/1/1 or more The caster rolls on the major magical mishap 
table. 
▶ Success: All dead humanoid bodies within a mile of the caster 
rise as zombies under the caster’s control. The zombies slavishly 
follow the caster’s command but do not count as one of the caster’s 
followers as determined by the rules for Personality. The caster 
gains 1d4+1 grime points and must spend 6FP for this spell to cast. 
This spell can only be cast once per month. 
▶ 6/6/6/6/6 or more: All dead humanoid bodies within 10 miles of 
the caster rise as zombies under the caster’s control. The zombies 
slavishly follow the caster’s command but do not count as one of the 
caster’s followers as determined by the rules for Personality. This 
spell can only be cast once per month. The caster gains 1d4+1 grime 
points and must spend 14FP for this spell to cast. This effect replaces 
the normal success effect. 
 
 


 
79 
 
Magical Items
Magic Items in MB&R 
Magic items such as powerful swords imbued with sorcery are a 
staple of the fantasy genre. In Mud, Blood & Rats’ sister game -
ADVANCED FANTASY which provides rules for high fantasy 
adventure in the more traditional genre heroic genre - there is 
almost an expectation that player characters will quickly accrue 
magic items that they will use to augment their power and 
capabilities. This is not the case in Mud, Blood & Rats.  
 
Due to the intrinsically inimical nature of magic in the grubby 
fantasy genre, magic items play a very different role. Firstly, they 
are much rarer, and many characters will never gain access to 
powerful magic items. Secondly, they are often as dangerous to the 
bearer as their would-be enemies. Therefore, this chapter deals 
with some magical items that a character may come across, as well 
as some details as how they are used and where they might come 
from.  
 
Example Magic Items 
Below you will find examples of magic items. 
 
Bane Weapon 
This magic weapon is particularly good against certain enemies. Roll 
on the table below to determine what enemy type the weapon is a 
slayer of. 
Qualities 
▶ Augment Power: The melee weapon gains the rend (2) quality 
against a specific type of enemy, listed below and doubles its 
damage against such  enemies. If the weapon already has a rend 
quality, then this increases its value by 2. 
▶ Possible Enemy Types: The enemy types that a bane weapon 
may affect are listed here. Each bane weapon may only ever affect 
one type of enemy. The possible types are: 
 
Dragons 
 
Goblinoids 
 
Undead 
Dangers and Hazards 
Whenever a wielder of a bane weapon can see the enemy type for 
which the weapon is a bane, they must save vs PER or attempt to 
attack the creature until it is dead. 
 
 
 
Blade of Dancing Metal 
A blade of dancing metal is often favoured by the artificers of the 
elf lands. It consists generally of a type of sword that is perfectly 
balanced. The blade of the weapon, unlike normal swords, is not 
made of iron or steel and is instead either copper, silver or gold 
which is then reinforced magically. 
Qualities 
▶ Copper: The weapon grants the wielder one extra attack as a 
free action per round. This weapon is rare, often used by elvish 
wizards and dukes. 
▶ Silver: The weapon grants the wielder two extra attacks as a 
free action per round. This weapon is exceptionally rare, perhaps 
only reserved for royalty of the elven kingdoms. 
▶ Gold: The weapon grants the wielder three extra attacks as a 
free action per round. It is uniquely rare to find a blade of dancing 
gold, and it may be that no one has ever managed to make one. 
Dangers and Hazards 
Like the elven smiths that often forge them, blades of dancing 
metal are mercurial and imbued with fickle energy. Any critical 
failure made on an attack test with a blade of dancing metal 
automatically cause a hit at full damage on the wielder (instead of 
the usually critical fumble roll as the weapon flickers about 
impossibly fast. 
Mage’s Staff 
Constructed by wizards and mages, staffs can be used to augment 
their power and control the flows of arcane energy they summon. 
Many wizard guilds will have these for purchase, or at least their 
component parts. A mage’s staff usually costs around 30+2d10gp to 
purchase. 
Qualities 
▶ Augment Power: The staff grants the wielder +1 sorcery dice.  
Dangers and Hazards 
All spells cast by the magician gain the following spell entry, in 
addition to any others: 
1/1/1/1: The caster gains a point of grime. 
 
Malusite 
Malusite is a silver ore that reflects with lurid purple light. It is 
found deep in the core of the world or as fragments of passing 
stars. It has many names such as madness ore, moon rock, and 
philosopher’s stone. Malusite is dangerous to be near: while it can 
be used to augment a magician’s power, it also radiates corruption. 
Qualities 
▶ Dust: When this dust is consumed, it grants the consumer an 
extra two successes the next time they cast a spell. 
▶ Nugget: When this nugget is consumed, it grants the consumer 
an extra three successes the next time they cast a spell. 
▶ Lump: When this lump is consumed, it grants the consumer an 
extra four successes the next time they cast a spell. 
Dangers and Hazards 
Malusite bleeds evil into the world. If it is stored in a lead-lined 
container then much of this corrupting energy is contained. But if 
it is handled directly or consumed then it afflicts its consumer with 
grime points as directed below: 
▶ Dust: Causes the consumer to gain 1d4 grime points. A 
character handling it openly gains one grime point per day. 
▶ Nugget: Causes the consumer to gain 1d4+2 grime points. A 
character handling it openly gains one grime point per hour. 
▶ Lump: Causes the consumer to gain 1d6+6 grime points. A 
character handling it openly gains one grime point per round. 
 
 
 
Moon Metal Weapons 
Moon metal weapons, sometimes called mithril, aren’t true magic 
items but instead formed from fragments of meteoric and moon 
iron. They are favoured by the dwarfs.  
Qualities 
▶ Pierce Armour: The melee weapon gains the rend (1) quality. If 
the weapon already has a rend quality, then this increases its value 
by 1.  
Dangers and Hazards 
A character must be of at least Expert STR to wield a moon-metal 
weapon. 
 
 


 
80 
 
Rune-Forged Armour 
Master artificers of the dwarfs and, even more rarely, the elves can 
sometimes imbue armour with magical protections by inscribing 
arcane runes upon their surface. 
Qualities 
▶ Power Save: The piece of armour must be made of metal and 
includes shields. Depending on the strength of the rune, the 
wearer gains a power save to all hit locations as determined 
below. 
▶ 6+: The armour or shield grants the wearer a 6+ power save on 
all hit locations. This is very rare outside dwarf and elf lands and is 
often used by dwarf princelings. It has a value of hundreds of gold 
pieces. 
▶ 5+: The armour or shield grants the wearer a 5+ power save on 
all hit locations. This is virtually unheard of outside the dwarf 
realm and is practically only available for dwarf kings. It is too 
valuable to ever be for sale. 
▶ 4+: The armour or shield grants the wearer a 4+ power save on 
all hit locations. Even amongst dwarfs, this is exceptionally rare 
and would be guarded jealously by members of that race. 
Dangers and Hazards 
There are no hazards or dangers associated with rune-forged 
armour but one must accept that in most situations it is crafted for 
dwarf-sized persons. Similarly, any non-dwarf wearing rune-forged 
armour will be the subject of extreme jealousy by dwarfs who will 
almost certainly accuse the wearer of murder and theft. 
 
Scroll of Dispel Magic 
These magical scrolls can help a wizard block any enemy 
magicians’ spells before they begin. 
Qualities 
▶ Dispel Magic: When a magician attempts to counterspell, they 
may consume this scroll to grant +2 successes to their counterspell 
attempt. 
Dangers and Hazards 
If the scroll is consumed and the counterspell still fails, then the 
wielder gains 2 grime points. 
 
 
The Sword of Midnight 
The Sword of Midnight has many names: the Doom of the Dawn, 
the Soul Drinker or simply ‘The Black Sword’. No one knows 
where it came from or what its true purpose in this world is. It is 
simply known that whoever wields the sword will be possessed of 
awesome power yet cursed with unavoidable tragedy.  
Qualities 
▶ Augment Power: The wielder gains a +6 bonus to all melee 
attack tests. The weapon causes 10 slashing damage, rend (4). The 
wielder gains resistance to every type of damage and critically hits 
with this weapon on a 17, 18, 19, or 20.  
Dangers and Hazards 
While wielding this weapon the bearer is assaulted by whispered 
threats and supernatural mockery. If they die while wielding the 
sword, it drinks their soul. Similarly, every time it is placed back in 
its sheath, the wielder gains a grime point. Every time the wielder 
has an extended rest following a day in which they have not killed 
a humanoid, demon or goblinoid using the sword, they awake with 
3 drained conditions. 
 
 
 
 
 
 


 
81 
 
Game Mastery
Your Role as Game Master 
If you are reading this section, then it is probably because you have 
at least a partial capacity for adventure and dream of laying out a 
grand design of hazards and challenges for your friends to work 
through within the confines of a grubby fantasy setting. It is also 
more than likely that you yourself have a great sense of the sort of 
tale you wish to let your players tell as they go from absolute 
unknowns to relative unknowns in a grim world of slimy peril and 
inimical horror. One thing is certain though, that you inhabit a 
privileged position as game master, a role that you no doubt take 
seriously. 
 
Evoking the ‘Grubbiness’ in ‘Grubby’ Fantasy 
Mud, Blood & Rats is a game of grubby fantasy in which ordinary 
people face anything but ordinary threats in a fantasy world that 
feels very much ‘lived in’. Beyond the confines of the walled cities 
of the world lies a vast wilderness inhabited by dark creatures and 
vile, scheming cultists. At first glance it may seem that even the most 
civilised and safe areas of such a setting could at any time be 
overwhelmed by the evil forces and monstrous nightmare-things that 
inhabit the world. This is even more true in the rural settlements, 
that act as tiny pinpricks of light within the hundreds of miles of 
beast-haunted forests and bleak flinty mountain passes. 
 
Grubby fantasy is not, however, simply dark fantasy. While the 
above may seem true from the perspective of the player characters, 
there are in fact many capable and even powerful people working 
on the side of good and order, trying desperately to not only resist, 
but to push back the ever-coming tide of darkness. To run a game 
in such a setting is to present your players with an even truer sense 
of heroism than most games ever achieve: even ADVANCED 
FANTASY, the high fantasy ruleset for Journeyman, Expert, Master, 
and sister to Mud, Blood & Rats, in allowing for capable and less 
imperilled characters creates an atmosphere where heroism is easy. 
Surely it is the case that a ragtag group of rat catchers, beggars and 
peasants choosing to stand and fight against lumbering behemoth 
monsters is in fact more heroic than an elite group of fighters, clerics 
and bards doing the same? 
 
To help you as the game master evoke the correct atmosphere of 
such a game, you are reminded to review the tenets of grubby 
fantasy laid out in the introduction of this book as often as you are 
able. But you are also encouraged to consider the following, which 
are more practical than those guiding tenets:  
 
▶ Make the world feel lived in: The world of a grubby fantasy 
setting is considerably more tangible than that of high fantasy. 
Encourage players to ask about the facilities when they enter a 
tavern to rent rooms for the night, draw attention to the fact that the 
gate guard asks where they’ll be staying but that he seems 
disinterested and really wants to get back to his game of cards. 
Details like this can help flesh out the world (the character sheet 
contains a section for the number of days since the character last 
had a wash for a reason!).  
▶ Remind them of the presence of grime: If the characters behave 
in a way that is morally base, or if they encounter grimy influences 
then they are going to gain grime points. It is not your job to try and 
force them into situations where they feel they must gain grime 
points for rerolls of tests, but it is your job to remind them that they 
can do this. It is also your job to remind them that being a mutant 
invites difficult questions from polite society, and that many such 
unfortunate people are burnt at the stake. 
▶ Remind them of the presence of grit: The characters are 
destined; this is why they have grit points. Grit points allow them to 
attempt (and perhaps even achieve) truly heroic deeds in an 
otherwise largely apathetic world. You are encouraged to remind 
them of this, and perhaps, in extreme situations, even bait them with 
the promise of more grit points if they endeavour to complete 
particularly heroic acts. 
▶ Reinforce social mores: It is likely that the characters do not have 
particularly high status, at least at the start of a campaign. You 
should emphasise this to them, ensuring that they do not blunder 
wildly into the places of power demanding to be heard as doing so 
is likely to get them humiliated, arrested, or worse. 
 
Using Character Traits 
All characters in Mud, Blood & Rats have three traits listed on their 
sheet. These are their virtue, vice and ambition. Each of these can 
be leaned upon in order to leverage the grubby atmosphere of the 
game and it is important that you encourage your players to refer 
to them and engage with them. If characters lean into these traits, 
then they should be rewarded experience points, as detailed 
below. 
 
Virtue 
Virtues are what drives a character. In the context of the game, a 
virtue refers to the character’s guiding benevolent principles. 
Virtues may be particular bonds, desires, or morals that the 
character has. Examples might include: 
 
▶ I am a faithful person and believe that the gods do truly watch 
over us. I will embody my faith where I can. 
▶ My twin is a part of me. I will look after her until one of use dies. 
▶ I am willing to die for my friends. 
 
If a player expresses their character’s virtue strongly during a session 
of Mud, Blood & Rats then they should be rewarded with a low 
miscellaneous experience point reward appropriate to their 
character’s level. A character should only ever receive this reward a 
maximum of once per session (or every four ours, whichever is 
shorter). Additional examples are found in the appendices. 
 
Vice 
A vice is a negative character quality that represents the very worst 
of a character. Examples of a character vice may include: 
 
▶ I cannot stand people who believe in different gods to me. I will 
have them burned. 
▶ I love wine and ale. I love it so much that each day that passes 
without them I grow more and more hostile to everyone. 
▶ Gold is the only thing that makes me happy. 
 
If a player expresses their character’s vice strongly during a session 
of Mud, Blood & Rats then they should be rewarded with an average 
miscellaneous experience point reward appropriate to their 
character’s level. A character should only ever receive this reward a 
maximum of once per session (or every four hours, whichever is 
shorter). It may seem strange that a character should receive more 
experience points for leaning into a vice than a virtue. To this, you 
are reminded that this is a grim and grubby game in which heroism 
is slightly scarcer. Additional examples are found in the appendices. 
 
Ambition 
An ambition is a trait that will change frequently during a 
campaign of Mud, Blood & Rats. This represents a character’s most 
immediate desire and what they are trying to achieve. It may relate 
to themselves or the group. Generally, an ambition must be 
decided with the game master and should be something that is 
achieved roughly every 2-4 sessions of play. Examples may 
include: 
▶ I am a witch hunter and therefore I must seek out a warlock and 
burn him. 
▶ As a wizard’s apprentice, I desperately want to cast my first spell. 
▶ The town guard are all corrupt. I will remove the guard captain 
from his post. 
If a player achieves their character’s ambition strongly during a 
session of Mud, Blood & Rats then they should be rewarded with a 
high miscellaneous experience point reward appropriate to their 
character’s level. Additional examples are not available in the 
appendices as is the case with virtues and vices as ambitions should 
be specific to a game master’s campaign. 
 
 


Game Mastery 
82 
 
Tests 
The core mechanic of Mud, Blood & Rats is the attribute and skill 
test. A test is made by a character when the game master calls for it. 
To make a test, the character rolls 1d20 and compares the number 
to their listed Target Number for that level. If the result is equal to 
or higher than the Target Number, then they have succeeded.  
 
For example, Gundarr the Dwarf’s player wishes to break down a 
locked door using the Strength attribute. Gundarr is of 4th level and 
his Experience Path is the Jack. He has Journeyman proficiency, 
meaning he needs a 12 or more to successfully break down the door. 
 
Difficulty Levels  
Often a simple skill check against a character’s skill will suffice; 
however, there are times when the GM may wish to alter the chances 
of a skill being successfully used. Using the example above, it may 
be that the door Gundarr is trying to break down is made of rotten 
wood and is especially weak, or is particularly strong and bound in 
stout iron bands. In such cases, the game master may wish to adjust 
the difficulty of the check, in order to make it easier or harder to 
succeed. A simple, unmodified skill check is referred to as 
Challenging but there are other difficulty levels, listed below, which 
provide modifiers to the Test roll: 
 
DIFFICULTY LEVELS 
Difficulty 
Modifier to Roll 
Routine 
+9 
Very Easy 
+6 
Easy 
+3 
Challenging 
- 
Hard 
-3 
Very Hard 
-6 
Formidable 
-9 
 
Opposed Tests 
Sometimes a creature will end up in an opposed test with another 
creature, such as a warrior trying to push an ogre off a cliff using 
Strength or a wizard in a mental dual with a foe using Intellect.  
 
When completing opposed tests, only one participant rolls the test, 
usually the creature that has used an action to initiate the opposed 
test (referred to on the table below as the ‘attacker’). To work out 
what the target number should be, consult the table below. 
 
Critical Successes and Failures 
When a character makes a test that is a natural 20 on the d20 roll, 
then they have scored a ‘critical’. Generally critical results are 
considered to have passed regardless of any other modifiers. 
Conversely, if a natural 1 is rolled then the test is considered to have 
failed regardless of any other modifiers. 
 
FP Flip and FP Burn 
Any character may choose to flip a test. This involves spending 1FP 
to reroll a failed test or save. A character can flip a failed test as 
many times as they like but they cannot flip a critical failure. 
 
As a character gains talents, they may be able to burn FP. This gives 
them a +1 bonus to any rolled test or save at the cost of 1FP. A 
character may burn as much FP as they like on a single test or save. 
For example, they may burn 5FP to gain a +5 bonus. A creature may 
burn FP on a test after the dice has been rolled, turning a failure 
into success. FP may never be spent to increase a critical failure. 
Similarly, spending FP to bring a test to a 20 does not count as a 
critical success. 
 
Armour saves, power saves, and sorcery dice cannot be flipped or 
burned without the appropriate talents. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 


Game Mastery 
83 
 
Dramatic Tests 
Sometimes it will be necessary to have a series of tests as part of a 
larger challenge. This is known as a Dramatic test. A Dramatic test 
consists of a number of rounds in which player characters can 
contribute to the overall success or failure of a particular moment. A 
Dramatic test works as follows: 
 
The game master explains the possible outcome and then states how 
many successful skill and attribute tests must be made to succeed, 
as well as how many failures the Dramatic test can sustain until it is 
considered failed overall.  
 
Each round a character may make one skill or attribute test in 
contribution to the overall Dramatic test outcome. The game master 
should be willing to suggest the relevant skills and attributes and 
how they may be useful to succeeding overall in a Dramatic test. 
Additionally, the game master should also be willing to take 
suggestions from players as to what other skills and attributes may 
be relevant.  
 
What is important is that once a skill is used successfully once in a 
dramatic test, then it cannot be used again. During a Dramatic test, 
each skill and attribute may only be successfully used once. 
 
An example Dramatic test is provided below. 
 
Example Dramatic Test: Lost in the Blizzard 
The characters have become lost in an arctic region. Instead of a 
simple survival test to find shelter, the game master decides to make 
this a Dramatic test as a blizzard rolls in.  
 
The game master states that to find shelter and safety from the 
blizzard the party will need to make 4 successful skill tests before 3 
failures. 
 
They explain that the relevant skills and attributes for this test are: 
▶ END: A character may test END in order to help their comrades 
grit their teeth and push on through the blizzard. 
▶ Perception: A character may test Perception to find appropriately 
dry firewood.  
▶ Lore (Nature): A character may test Lore (Nature) in order to 
pass on information about this region, such as where natural shelter 
like caves may be. 
▶ Pray (-6): A character may test Pray as very hard (-6) to ask their 
gods to abate the terrible weather. 
▶ Survival (+3): A character may test Survival as easy (+3) to scout 
an appropriate place to camp. 
▶ Pathfinding: A character may test Pathfinding to follow a game 
trail perhaps to the shelter that is used by a large mammal like a 
cave bear, or maybe to a trapper’s lodge. 
Players may of course suggest additional skills that a game master 
should consider. With each failure, the game master should 
introduce some hidden threat. In the above example it may provoke 
saves vs END, with characters suffering cold damage on a failure. If 
an overall success is achieved, then the Dramatic test should yield 
experience point rewards. An overall failure should result in 
significant setbacks and negative consequences. 
 
Dramatic tests can be used for any scenario that is complex and hard 
to manage mechanically. Examples may include: scaling a mountain, 
negotiating with a noble family, fighting for control of a sailing ship 
in a storm, escaping a burning city, or any other like scenario.
